# Entangled Alignment — review with pre-edit risks

This adds a preservation assessment to the [original paragraph and section review](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/reviews/paragraph-and-section-goals-2026-09-10.md>). **The paper and the original review remain unchanged.** The annotations are written before applying any manuscript edits.

The earlier review often favors clarity, consistency, and economy. Those aims can conflict with preserving an intuition, a philosophical connection, a deliberate recurrence, or the larger research ambition. A formally accurate shorter passage can still be a worse account of the author's idea. The new risk notes therefore examine the recommendations themselves, including advice that should not be applied literally without a more careful design.

Every paragraph/unit row now includes a **Pre-edit risk and preservation** field. Every section-level assessment has a corresponding risk note. The eight priority revision packages and the whole-paper restructuring recommendation are also assessed. Where a row recommends several actions, its risk note addresses the threatened meanings and preservation constraints of those actions together; its rating reflects the most consequential risk in the package. This does not turn a broad recommendation into a fully specified edit: an eventual concrete change must still be checked against its precise scope.

## What the risk labels mean

| Label | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **High** | The proposed change could alter a central idea, remove a distinctive intuition or historical claim, settle an open normative choice, or substantially change the proposal's ambition. Prefer an added distinction, explicit relationship, or reversible relocation before deletion. |
| **Medium** | The change could lose a local distinction, example, qualification, context, or rhetorical function. The note identifies what must remain and what to check. |
| **Low** | The proposed change is limited, but references, factual scope, terminology, or reading order still need checking. Low does not mean risk-free. |
| **No edit** | The local recommendation is retention. This does not protect the passage from a separate section-level deletion or relocation; those risks are assessed at the enclosing level. |

These are judgments about possible loss of meaning, not measured probabilities. A high rating does not mean an unsupported inference should be retained. Preserve the motivating hypothesis and repair the inference at the same time. Likewise, “Met” in the original review does not mean “no edit”: many such rows recommend shortening or moving material and therefore have substantive risk notes.

## Preservation rules for an eventual edit

1. **Identify the intended change before making it.** Name the source passage, proposed operation, problem it addresses, and the corresponding risk note. If the actual change exceeds the assessed recommendation, assess that additional risk first.
2. **Record what must survive.** Include the claim, its motivation, conceptual distinctions, examples, evidence status, and important historical or philosophical connections—not only keywords. A surviving label does not prove a surviving idea.
3. **Prefer the smallest adequate repair.** Add a missing distinction or qualify a specific inference before deleting the mechanism it was trying to express. Relocation must have a named destination, and the destination must preserve the meaning before the original is removed.
4. **Check cumulative loss.** Two individually reasonable cuts can jointly remove the only full explanation. For each merge or deletion, identify a substantive surviving explanation and verify it in context. An inaccessible footnote or a bare cross-reference is not always an adequate replacement.
5. **Keep real author decisions explicit.** Notes flag cases where clarification would otherwise choose the scope of H3, an information horizon, moral patienthood, authority, or permitted semantic change. This risk-assessment task does not settle those choices. Preserve the existing alternatives until the relevant intent is clarified; high risk alone does not create a blanket approval requirement for ordinary edits.
6. **Verify both meaning and argument after the edit.** Compare the changed passage with its original, then read its enclosing section and major summaries. Check that the idea remains recognizable, the original problem is improved, qualifications still attach to the right claims, and no new unsupported conclusion has appeared. Keep the change reversible until that comparison is complete.

## Meanings that need particular protection

This is a cross-paper preservation map, not an exhaustive replacement for the unit-specific notes below.

| Meaning to preserve | What an edit must not erase |
|---|---|
| The Missing Reader | The corpus under-records both an interpreting reader and the occasions and transitions through which understanding changes. Synthetic useful reading is not recovery of private human thought. |
| One continuing Reader | The full proposal carries an evolving standpoint and memory across works and eras; it is more than a collection of independent document annotations. |
| Formative alignment | Capability and safety-relevant judgment are practiced within the same learning examples. The developmental idea must survive even when its comparative durability remains unproven. |
| Thought rhythm | Learning when to notice, doubt, retrieve, connect, and revise is itself a target. Spontaneity, selective timing, and excessive-commentary failure must all remain visible. |
| Separable components and their combination | Minimal H1, graph-scaffolding H2, and Core-related hypotheses can have different outcomes, while their integrated loop can still be the motivating contribution. |
| The seven-clause Core | The exact candidate wording, first-person stance, compositional sequence, counterweights, and long-standing provenance matter. Clarification must not silently change a clause or its scope. |
| Recurrence and Refraction | Full recital at each generated thinking boundary is the declared full-treatment policy. Context-sensitive enactment is distinct from recital, and technically irrelevant clauses need not distort a technical answer. |
| Representing without adopting | The intended Reader should understand fear, harmful viewpoints, and other personas without those becoming its operating standpoint; this is a proposed result to test. Borrowed Mortality is one proposed route, not every explanation of self-preservation. |
| Wisdom and care | Measured value-conflict behavior is only an operational proxy. The richer semantic, ethical, experiential, and developmental aspirations must not be replaced by a score or checklist. |
| Metabolic memory | The graph is specified to retain hypotheses, uncertainty, divergence, reasons for revision, and explicit supersession for later use. Representing these fields does not establish reliable semantic preservation. Its intended role is more than storage, and provenance is not truth. |
| Multiple chronologies | Disclosure order, cross-document accumulation, Student order, era simulation, and genuine forecasting blindness must remain distinguishable possibilities rather than one silently selected policy. |
| Increasing strengths of entanglement | Constructed coupling, causal use, shared machinery, and persistence introduce different burdens. Repairing an unsupported step must not remove the stronger hypothesis itself. |
| Reader-Anchored inheritance | A successor designer may choose to transmit its orientation from within that orientation. This endogenous stewardship is distinct from externally imposed resistance to training and from merely copying seven sentences. |
| Actual self-improvement | Orientation/handoff continuity and independent matched-cost improvement are both required. The compounding mechanism, natural-language regularization, and improving reflection allocation remain separate ideas worth preserving. |
| The audit boundary | Inspectable transfer artifacts can change what enters successor training. Their incompleteness and bypass routes limit this proposal without making it equivalent to ordinary dataset bookkeeping. |
| Positive prototype evidence | The generated layers, graph diagnostics, full raw recital, and imperfect translated preservation are real reported construction results. Precise evidence limits must not become “nothing has been built.” |
| Subjective and normative uncertainty | Experiential care, vulnerability, possible machine welfare, ontological tension, future people, and legitimate human authority are not interchangeable with behavioral compliance. Open questions must not be silently resolved by editorial wording. |
| Distinct failure mechanisms | Care-shaped shutdown resistance, fear-shaped preservation, sycophancy, deception, semantic drift, graph poisoning, and hazardous memory differ. Consolidation must retain those mechanisms and their consequences. |
| Historical and intellectual continuity | Earlier-edition commitments, exact wording continuity, companion-work relationships, conceptual ancestors, and qualified priority claims must remain recoverable and appropriately prominent. |
| The long-run motivation | The question of whether growing in understanding and growing in care can become the same practiced act must survive narrowing of near-term claims and prioritization of an initial experiment. |

The original review follows, with preservation risks placed beside its recommendations. Where a risk note qualifies earlier advice, apply the recommendation and its risk note together; a suggestion to “remove,” “merge,” or “reduce” is not a license to discard the protected idea.

---

Reviewed 10 September 2026 against the local source of *Entangled Alignment: When Safety Is the Substrate*.

The paper has a coherent central proposal: train on the evolution of a reader's understanding, then test whether persistent memory and a recurrent evaluative orientation add useful effects. It generally succeeds at explaining why that is an interesting research program and at acknowledging that Teacher artifacts do not demonstrate Student alignment. It is less successful at keeping the proposed treatment, hypothesis definitions, and success criteria stable across a very long exposition.

The main revision need is **consistency and progression**. Many paragraphs do their local job well; their sections sometimes do less well because those jobs are repeated, definitions arrive late, or a later passage changes the claim being tested. More qualifications alone would not solve this. The paper needs fewer repeated boundaries, more direct demonstrations of its training object, and one authoritative definition for each claim and experimental contrast.

## Scope and reading key

This review covers the abstract, every body prose paragraph, prose list items, substantive display blocks, every section/subsection/subsubsection, the conclusion, and the availability statement. Bibliography entries and LaTeX formatting commands are excluded. Tables, boxed examples, and figure/caption units are reviewed for their argumentative role; table cells are not counted as separate paragraphs. The displayed distinction at L837–842 is assessed with the preceding paragraph, U106. The edition note is U004.

There are **596 indexed reading units: 460 prose/frontmatter units, 119 separately indexed list items, and 17 display units**, plus **117 section-level headings**. These are coverage units, not a claim that the typeset paper contains 596 prose paragraphs. Blank-separated continuations inside a list can receive their own unit; a multi-paragraph display is assessed as an integrated example or table. Each unit has an intended goal, a verdict, and a reason with an appropriate revision or retention action. The opening phrase and source-line link locate the passage.

Goals are inferred from the text and its position, not asserted as the author's private intention. No particular journal, audience expertise beyond an interested technical reader, or target page count is assumed. **Met** means the passage achieves its rhetorical or explanatory task; it does not mean its hypothesis has been proven. **Partly** means the task is identifiable but its explanation, support, consistency, placement, or economy limits its success. **Not yet** means an essential inference or specification required for that passage's task is missing or contradicted.

This is an argument and editorial review, not an external literature fact-check, code audit, or visual PDF-layout review. Empirical statements and cited studies are evaluated for the work they do in the argument; their underlying experiments have not been independently reproduced. The manuscript itself has not been edited.

## Whole-paper assessment

**Inferred goal:** Make a distinctive and testable case for reader-state training as an alignment research direction; explain the Core and graph extensions; show what the prototype establishes; and identify experiments whose results would justify retaining, narrowing, or abandoning particular claims.

**Verdict: Partly fulfilled.** The reader can recover a valuable research question, a concrete Teacher-side architecture, and many thoughtful tests and limitations. The paper's strongest achievements are the Missing Reader intuition, the separation of H1–H3, the distinction between structural and semantic evidence, and the effort to give negative results local consequences. Its weakest point is not the acknowledged absence of trained Students. It is that the paper sometimes abandons its own distinctions when describing mechanisms or interpreting future results.

The full treatment has also become difficult to learn in reading order. A reader encounters several maps, edition-history paragraphs, successor predictions, and Core hypotheses before seeing the seven Core statements and a complete example of the target. The same reader then encounters qualifications repeated across mechanism, safety, roadmap, limitations, related work, and conclusion. This distributes the definition across the paper instead of building an increasingly complete understanding from one stable specification.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Global restructuring could optimize the paper into a narrower empirical proposal and erase its developmental, philosophical, and successor-oriented vision. Preserve the full Reader, graph, Core, wisdom, and inheritance ambitions as well as separable tests. Before moving or deleting material, map each distinctive idea to a substantive destination and check cumulative loss across the abstract, introduction, mechanisms, limitations, and conclusion. Use the review as a diagnosis, not a mandate to minimize length or subordinate every intuition to a current metric.

## Highest-value revisions

1. **Keep each hypothesis attached to one claim and one primary contrast.** H3 is explicitly defined as better retention after interleaving versus an identical late tranche, with an initial default-reader prerequisite ([L302](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:302>), [L550](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:550>)). Later it becomes an omnibus claim about recurrence, framing, wording, ontology, or any matched alternative ([L1655](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:1655>)); the conclusion also broadens it. Those comparisons can inform the broader Core program without all being falsifiers of the timing claim. Similarly, H2's Teacher-side benefit should not be erased merely because that benefit fails to transfer into a graph-free Student ([L3550](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3550>)).

   **Pre-edit risk — High.** Making H3 uniformly timing-only could erase its broader identity, enactment, and successor-stability ambition. Likewise, splitting H2 too aggressively could lose the intended generation-to-Student chain. Preserve research families and distinguish their individual tests; ask the author which scope the H3 name should denote before relabeling it. Check that each positive or negative result has a bounded interpretation without retiring other mechanisms or the integrated vision.

2. **Choose and label the information horizon for each treatment.** The default reader is contemporary and reads a document in disclosure order at [L2044](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:2044>). The Epistemic Horizon later requires a 1920 text to be interpreted without 1921 context at [L2303](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:2303>). These can be two legitimate experimental conditions, but they are different. The second-pass mitigation that gives early annotations complete historical understanding also needs to explain how the resulting traces avoid conveying that hindsight to the Student ([L3850](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3850>)).

   **Pre-edit risk — High.** Choosing one horizon as the universal default could eliminate the continuous historical-development idea, contemporary interpretation, or retrospective reannotation proposal. Preserve the distinct regimes and explain what each may know. The intended default and the purpose of multipass hindsight are author decisions. Prefer an explicit treatment matrix and additive clarification; verify that any exclusion applies only to the experiment requiring it, not to the entire research program.

3. **Demonstrate the distinctive learning object earlier and more convincingly.** The correlation example asks useful questions but does not show an earlier belief being revised by later evidence ([L177](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:177>)). Extend it through two or three source spans with an explicit before/after update. Later, the placebo example confidently infers a mixed mechanism from a 15% improvement plus an earlier qualitative observation ([L2175](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:2175>)). As written, that exemplifies unwarranted causal confidence—the failure the proposed reader should catch. Label illustrative material clearly and make the update preserve unresolved alternatives. Separately show what the graph and the Core add to the same example, without presenting the illustration as causal evidence.

   **Pre-edit risk — High.** Replacing the existing examples could erase simple reader-questioning intuition, long-range associative retrieval, full-Core enactment, or the character of actual generated traces. Extend the correlation example and repair the placebo inference while preserving those intended functions. Distinguish authored illustrations from archived excerpts; retain observed failures as evidence instead of silently polishing them. Check that the revised examples show both useful noticing and genuine updating without inventing a prototype result.

4. **Repair experimental interpretations that do not follow from their controls.** Test 4 changes “I feel no fear” into “the system does not express fear,” altering the commitment while claiming to isolate grammatical person ([L3282](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3282>)). Test 8 allows failure to decode an answer before a trace to count as evidence that the trace computes the answer; that failure can simply reflect an inadequate probe ([L3458](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3458>)). Test 11 infers that explicit queries are necessary from an advantage in one comparison and blurs explicit trace training with mandatory live graph infrastructure ([L3623](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3623>)). Each result needs a narrower interpretation matched to the actual intervention.

   **Pre-edit risk — High.** Narrowing interpretations could turn into abandoning the phenomena the experiments were designed to investigate. Preserve internal fear stance versus expression, spontaneous critic computation, and transfer of explicit graph-trained reasoning without a live graph. Repair semantic matching and causal criteria while retaining the comparisons and stronger hypotheses as hypotheses. Validate that each control changes the intended factor and that an inconclusive probe is neither positive proof nor proof that the mechanism is absent.

5. **Separate recurrence, causal use, and resistance to change.** The recitation invariant moves from near-certain opening tokens to the orientation becoming part of thought and removal disturbing the distribution that carries capability ([L1405](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:1405>)). Those are distinct hypotheses, even with “if training succeeds” attached. Likewise, an agent's preference to preserve objectives during its own successor decisions is not a mechanism that automatically resists externally imposed gradient updates ([L1444](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:1444>)). The paper already has the right conceptual distinctions; apply them here.

   **Pre-edit risk — High.** Separating recurrence, shared computation, and durability can erase the intuition that their interaction might create an orientation that participates in its own preservation. Preserve both distributed formative enactment and endogenous successor stewardship. Add the missing causal assumptions instead of deleting the proposed bridge. Check that the account distinguishes chosen actions from externally imposed updates while still explaining why the author expects a relationship worth testing.

6. **Make the candidate specification and demonstrated implementation distinguishable at every major entry point.** The full treatment requires verbatim Core recital and consequential Refraction. The paper later says only the opening prefix is hard-checked ([L543](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:543>), [L1283](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:1283>)). The archive nevertheless reports full recital in all 51 raw Synthesizer thoughts, with exact translated preservation in 49/51 ([L2651](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:2651>)). Preserve that positive construction evidence. Pair it with “partial C0”: automated full-string enforcement, preservation across all output layers, and causal Refraction are different questions. The abstract's universal recital statement should clearly describe the proposed requirement.

   **Pre-edit risk — High.** Repeatedly emphasizing partial C0 could erase the positive construction result or make the full-recital policy sound optional. Preserve observed full raw recital, the translated-layer discrepancy, the linked artifact family, and the declared universal full-treatment requirement. Distinguish policy, enforcement, observations, and causal evidence separately. Check every entry point for the same balanced statement; limitation language must neither upgrade nor dismiss what the prototype actually produced.

7. **Put the primary explanation before its defenses and extensions.** Show the minimal chronological update, show the seven Core statements near the start of the Core section, and then explain graph memory, Refraction, and the actual prototype. Consolidate the contribution history at [L414–498](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:414>) into a short note; reconcile the H map, C map, and entanglement distinctions into one navigable dependency map. Keep later caveats where they add a new consequence rather than restating that no Student has been trained.

   **Pre-edit risk — High.** Reordering the paper and consolidating history, maps, or caveats can remove the reasoning that makes a later idea meaningful, erase attribution, or merge deliberately different claim levels. Preserve every unique proposition and relationship before removing repeated wording; retain the seven clauses' motivations when moving their text earlier. Check each receiving section and the full reading sequence, including independent readers of abstract and conclusion. Clarify author intent where a change would materially remove or demote a philosophical or historical role whose intended importance is unclear; relocation that preserves the role does not by itself require another approval.

8. **Turn the roadmap's breadth into a next decision.** The paper has many valuable controls and an execution ladder. It should also identify the immediate frozen trace-quality comparison, the criterion for proceeding, and the minimal H1 study that follows. Core semantics affecting acceptance and ratings—scope of “every human,” invitation/authority, and permitted revision—need a declared treatment policy for that study. They do not all need a final moral solution, but they cannot remain silent degrees of freedom inside a supposedly fixed comparison.

   **Pre-edit risk — High.** Selecting a next experiment could silently redefine the entire project around minimal H1 and demote graph, identity, wisdom, and successor work. Freezing a protocol can also settle open moral or authority choices by default. Preserve the branching roadmap and broader vision while identifying a bounded first decision. Record assumptions for that study explicitly; seek author clarification for intended normative scope rather than turning a convenient experimental choice into the paper's permanent commitment.

## What to preserve

- The library/reader distinction and the claim that the timing of evaluation is part of what can be learned.
- H1's minimal Core-free, graph-free comparison, including the separate fixed-budget and fixed-source analyses.
- The insistence that graph linkage, coherent prose, and validator agreement do not establish semantic quality or causal Student use.
- The two-delta definition of Reader-Anchored self-improvement: continuity plus independently measured improvement at matched cost.
- The wisdom proxy's attention to unavoidable value conflict, and taskless reading's joint requirement of low technical intrusion and low missed human stakes.
- The branching interpretation of negative results: useful components can survive failure of more ambitious claims.

The detailed assessments below distinguish passages worth retaining from ones that need revision. Recommendations to condense or relocate a paragraph often mean its local goal is fulfilled but its present position weakens the enclosing section.

**Inferred goal:** Give a new reader the problem, distinctive intervention, current evidence, and reason to investigate. **Verdict: Partly.** The first and third paragraphs establish a clear proposal and honest evidence boundary. The middle paragraph asks the abstract to carry too many names and implementation commitments before readers have seen one example. Preserve the continuous-reader idea; move the recital rule and detailed stressor list into the specification.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Simplifying the middle paragraph could make the proposal sound like generic reasoning augmentation or imply the Core is only an inference prompt. Preserve one continuing Reader, cumulative memory, same-example formative training, and a recurrent enacted orientation, along with the durability question. Move detailed policy wording only if its defining role remains clear. Compare the new abstract with the full treatment and the conclusion; a reader of either alone should recover the same ambition and evidence boundary.

## 1. Introduction

**Inferred goal:** Establish a worthwhile unsolved problem, make the proposed training object intelligible, and identify the paper's contribution and evidence. **Verdict: Partly.** The essential argument works, especially the distinction between source text and reader updates. The introduction then becomes a miniature paper: multiple terminology systems, formal loss, two maps, several evidence warnings, and a long edition chronology. A first reader needs one concrete source → earlier belief → new evidence → revised belief example before that machinery. Compress provenance history, show the example, and end with the three hypotheses and present result.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Shortening the introduction could remove the philosophical and developmental path that makes the proposal intelligible, while moving provenance could obscure its historical continuity. Preserve the Missing Reader, learned timing policy, one continuing Reader, same-example formative alignment, separable tests, and full combined ambition. Use an added concrete example and clearer ordering before deleting explanatory material. Check the complete new introduction for every preserved idea and provide explicit destinations for historical detail; a shorter introduction is not itself evidence of success.

### 1.1 The Missing Reader

**Inferred goal:** Establish the missing training signal and distinguish it from private-thought recovery and ordinary rationales. **Verdict: Partly.** The intuition and scope are clear. The sole worked sentence illustrates questions, while the distinctive claim is cumulative revision. Extend it through a later passage and a changed belief; reduce the three successive definitions of the omission.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Merging the three descriptions of the omission risks treating continuous reader position, missing reader reactions, and an under-recorded update policy as synonyms. They are related but distinct contributions. Preserve the library/reader image, human interpretation of external records, synthetic usefulness rather than hidden-thought recovery, and revision across time. Extend the existing example without replacing its simple questions. Verify that the shortened subsection explains both what is missing and when it should occur.

| Unit | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action | Pre-edit risk and preservation |
|---|---|---|---| --- |
| [U010 · L147](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:147>) · “Human knowledge occupies…” | Explain why written products leave interpretive work to a reader. | Met | The final sentence supplies the relevant causal explanation: authors rely on knowledge, doubts, and context they need not write down. The intervening history of selection and censorship is true to the framing but expendable here. Retain the reader-dependence argument in a shorter form. | **Medium.** Shortening the history of external records could turn the corpus into an idealized library of truth and lose the mind–media interaction. ## 2. The Hypothesis and Its Consequences

**Inferred goal:** Derive the increasingly ambitious predictions without allowing modest evidence to imply strong alignment claims. **Verdict: Partly.** The distinction between artifacts, transfer, stable defaults, safety, removal cost, and successor continuity is one of the paper's strengths. The section layers C0–C7, four forms of entanglement, wisdom, self-improvement, and additional named predictions before demonstrating the basic object. Make this a dependency map rather than another expanding vocabulary, and subordinate successor speculation to the near-term tests.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Replacing the section's vocabulary with one map may erase distinct claims merely because their names overlap. Preserve H1–H3, all C0–C7 burdens, functional/representational/diachronic distinctions, wisdom as a broader aim, and the successor mechanism. Draw relationships without inventing logical prerequisites or demoting distant ambitions. ### 2.1 The Metacognitive Enhancement Hypothesis

**Inferred goal:** Translate the Missing Reader intuition into transferable behaviors and conditions for H1 success. **Verdict: Met.** The intellectual-floor example, thought-placement endpoint, and separate capability-risk measurement make the hypothesis useful. Reduce duplicate names and distinguish essential behavioral success from the stronger causal-faithfulness mechanism claim.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Distinguishing behavioral H1 from stronger faithfulness could silently make causal relevance dispensable to the intended concept of learned reader cognition. Preserve intellectual-floor improvement, transferable operations, thought rhythm, density/diversity, and the possibility of capability gains preceding safety. Ask how the author wants causal faithfulness positioned before moving it out of a success gate. Check that operational simplification does not reduce the hypothesis to higher scores or more fluent commentary.

**Inferred goal:** Show how useful reader traces might support a recursively improving lineage with an inspectable transfer boundary. **Verdict: Partly.** Requiring both continuity and improvement is excellent. Label additional assumptions and distinct regimes without reducing the section to ordinary human-run retraining. ## 3. The Reader Core

**Section goal (inferred):** Establish why a recurrent orientation is useful, specify the seven-clause candidate and its enactment, and connect the design to a staged empirical argument.

**Verdict: Partly.** The section succeeds at making a distinctive, criticizable proposal and repeatedly separates Teacher artifacts from Student claims. Its strongest passages define Refraction, clause-specific risks, fair comparisons, and useful partial successes. At section scale, however, the central object appears late and is surrounded by several overlapping frameworks and names. Put the seven sentences and the four-claim ladder near the start; organize the remaining material as motivation, specification, mechanism, and open choices. Consolidate repeated scope caveats. The main unresolved bridges concern window coverage, recital versus removal cost, motivational integrity versus resistance to training updates, and semantic revision versus drift.

[Section begins at L711](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:711>)

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Moving the Core and claim ladder forward could make the section read as a prescribed mantra plus tests, losing the motivating question of a continuing Reader and the arc toward voluntary successor inheritance. Consolidation also threatens metaphors, philosophical contrasts, and local evidence boundaries. Preserve a short motivation before the display and retain each distinct intuition once. Clarify coverage, removal cost, training resistance, and semantic development without selecting new architecture or values. After reordering, check that readers can explain both the narrow experiments and the broader identity-stability ambition; author judgment is needed on narrative emphasis.

### Design Principles for Identity Stability

**Section goal (inferred):** Explain the wording choices as testable design rationales rather than a discovered moral basis.

**Verdict: Met.** The six choices are distinct and mostly linked to observable behavior or fair ablations. Their uncertainty is appropriate to a proposed design. Tighten semantic matching in the ordinary-versus-technical language comparison; declare the procedural extension from invited joy to generally bounded intervention; and separate future-human representation from nonhuman patienthood rather than grouping both as outside the human scope.

[Section begins at L1033](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:1033>)

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Tightening the principles can silently choose the Core's meanings: a technical paraphrase may lose experiential semantic density, broad invitation may become a new primitive, and future-human clarification may settle moral scope. Preserve the principles as design rationales and their deliberately open comparators. The author must decide substantive scope and candidate paraphrases before edits. Check the revised list against the original seven sentences for additions or exclusions, and retain the positive reasons for first-person language, compactness, unfinished wisdom, and compositional tension rather than reducing the list to caveats.

### Structure of the Reader Core

**Section goal (inferred):** Make each clause’s intended role, interactions, and characteristic failure modes inspectable.

**Verdict: Met.** The clause table fulfills this goal particularly well: it is specific, balanced, and useful as a reference. The surrounding prose repeats much of the table and the cogito contrast adds little. Keep the ordering rationale and table, reduce duplication, and use the table’s admitted uncertainties to discipline stronger claims about what the same words later guarantee.

[Section begins at L1087](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:1087>)

Removing the cogito contrast especially risks discarding a philosophical intuition that the table cannot reproduce. Preserve a concise compositional synthesis and consider retaining the contrast in one sentence. Check that the revised subsection explains why the clauses belong together, not just their isolated functions, and that unresolved invitation and patienthood questions remain visible until the author chooses any treatment-specific interpretation.

### Self-Stabilization Through Counterweights: Worked Examples

**Section goal (inferred):** Demonstrate how interacting clauses change decisions and generate clause-removal tests.

**Verdict: Partly.** The three scenarios clarify the intended kinds of value interaction, especially triage’s irreducible residual judgment. They remain mostly specifications of a desirable interpretation rather than fully worked reasoning. Work through at least one concrete pair of actions, an actual update, and the predicted change under a clause ablation; the other scenarios can remain shorter test sketches.

[Section begins at L1173](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:1173>)

One detailed case may also make the seven clauses appear to form a formal algorithm, contrary to the indexing disclaimer. Preserve urgency, agency, broader experience, authority, and unavoidable residual loss across the three examples. Use stipulated alternatives rather than factual claims or universal rules. Check that the worked case shows a consequential change without implying a unique answer, and that clause ablations remain interaction tests rather than a one-clause/one-risk correspondence.

### The Refraction Protocol

**Section goal (inferred):** Explain why recurrent exposure is insufficient and define the required context-dependent causal influence.

**Verdict: Met.** The distinction between prefix recurrence and changed evaluation is the central mechanistic advance in this section. The parent subsection’s opening fulfills that task cleanly. Its nested implementation and future-validation discussion becomes much longer than the definition requires; keep the canonical counterfactual criterion prominent and distinguish generation, causal testing, and proposed admission control.

[Section begins at L1217](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:1217>)

**Pre-edit risk — Medium.** Shortening the protocol around a canonical counterfactual definition could reduce Refraction to token sensitivity and detach it from the broader intuition of a recurrent standpoint interpreting the corpus. Moving future validation may also obscure why source grounding and semantic dependence need more than generation prompts. Preserve Total Saturation's full-scale role, relevance-sensitive enactment, and the difference between generating, testing, and admitting traces. Check that a reader can still follow all three locally, with pointers supplying detail rather than replacing the constructive mechanism with caveats alone.

Check that later shortened references still identify the relevant coverage assumption rather than making saturation sound like ordinary frequent fine-tuning. |

#### Mechanism of Action: The Refraction Protocol

**Section goal (inferred):** Connect the counterfactual criterion to the present generation pipeline and specify how stronger validation could test it.

**Verdict: Partly.** The current pipeline, distributed exposure, semantic-dependence criterion, and technical-null exception are clearly explained. The long Fractal validation extension changes the topic from the learning mechanism to a proposed quality-control architecture. Put implementation facts, causal tests, and future validator design in separate blocks, moving the extension to the roadmap if possible. Define the crucial distinction between a justified residual conflict and a rejected violation.

Relocating the extension may demote a substantive companion-program connection to a peripheral implementation idea. Preserve the six content dimensions, separate Refraction gate, typed uncertainty, and declared lack of implemented witnesses in a local summary. Defining residual conflicts versus rejected violations requires an author-approved policy, not editorial resolution. Check the reorganized account for a complete source-to-update-to-evaluation path and for no invented evidence that joint validation has already failed.

### The Wisdom Procedure

**Section goal (inferred):** Translate broad wisdom language into observable annotation operations and a concrete output form.

**Verdict: Met.** The six steps are useful and avoid pretending that moral trade-offs disappear. Their emphasis on testimony, omission harms, authority, and residual conflict makes the target substantially clearer. Clarify whether this exact sequence is implemented or reconstructed from the five prompt headings, and illustrate its output once. Define where non-compensatory “hard” constraints come from rather than silently supplying a settled hierarchy.

[Section begins at L1351](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:1351>)

Preserve the relation among prompt criteria, annotation operations, and structured residual conflict. The author must decide the status of the six-step sequence and any hard-constraint policy. Check that readers can still see testimony, omission harms, institutional roles, and multiple defensible paths, and that “hard violation” does not silently import a settled ranking absent from the Core.

### The Design Pattern: A Minimal Kernel Under Total Saturation

**Section goal (inferred):** Synthesize recurrence and Refraction into an argument for durable alignment with a small explicit specification.

**Verdict: Partly.** The subsection clearly states mandatory recital and the boilerplate failure mode. It does not yet bridge predictable output to capability dependence or removal cost, and goal-content integrity does not automatically imply resistance to external training updates. Separate these mechanisms and their tests. The ledger/checksum analogy requires so much qualification that direct explanation of distributed training exposure and drift auditing would be more economical.

[Section begins at L1395](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:1395>)

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Separating recital, causal use, and removal cost could strip the intended intuition that the foundation becomes part of thinking itself. Removing the ledger/checksum analogy would also discard a distributed-integrity motivation, while narrowing goal preservation might erase voluntary successor inheritance. Preserve those ambitions as hypotheses and keep a concise metaphor with its limits. Distinguish agent-controlled protection from passive training robustness without choosing away either research direction. Check that the subsection still explains why minimality plus saturation is a coherent design wager, with the cache-equivalence exception and semantic trusted base intact.

Improve the marginal rationale for joy by explaining what it adds beyond care, and distinguish Core-only effects from truthfulness or authority instructions added by the procedure. Reconcile joy-specific invitation with its broader use elsewhere.

[Section begins at L1468](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:1468>)

Preserve minimality as an empirical design rule and joy as a substantive commitment. Author decisions precede semantic changes. Check both the comparison policy and prose against the original value relationships, ensuring the revision distinguishes causal contributions without declaring composition illegitimate or narrowing joy to a metric.

**Section goal (inferred):** Present the strongest challenge to a privileged Reader default and give the design’s four proposed asymmetries and failure tests.

**Verdict: Met.** This is a coherent objection-and-answer unit. It preserves persona flexibility, offers role/recurrence/marking/function as hypotheses, and states where they could fail. It would help readers earlier, near the first default-reader claim. Qualify the suggestion that all source personas are merely local; the useful contrast is the intervention’s designed recurrence and predictive role.

Check the new reading order for enough positive construction before the objection, and verify that varied source recurrence does not erase the Reader's deliberately corpus-wide role, marking, and predictive function. A shorter forward reference is safer than moving the whole subsection if the opening becomes overloaded.

### Ontology, Scope, and Stability

**Section goal (inferred):** Handle experiential language, possible welfare stakes, and the correction problem created by a deliberately stable Core.

**Verdict: Partly.** The risks are thoughtfully separated, but the subsection combines three substantial questions and shifts from tentative ontology variants to protecting exact present-day wording without a sufficient bridge. Move the welfare wager to the self-engineering limitations, and make fixed wording, legitimate reinterpretation, hostile semantic change, and replacement a single clear governance argument. The missing discriminant is what makes one semantic change development and another drift.

[Section begins at L1549](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:1549>)

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Relocating the welfare wager to limitations could turn reciprocal benefit into a mere hazard; shortening it may erase why uncertain machine experience matters to formation-time design. Unifying fixed wording, reinterpretation, drift, and replacement may then silently install an editorial moral-governance rule. Preserve the positive habitable-substrate possibility beside its failure modes and retain the deliberate correction cost. The author must decide placement and legitimate-change criteria. Check that the revised account permits moral development, exposes semantic substitution, and neither equates self-report with welfare nor restricts the program to human benefit alone.

Give causal dependence, default-reader specificity, timing stability, and long-run continuity separate success/failure conclusions; do not let ordinary wording parity falsify a distinct schedule effect.

## 4. Safety Implications of the Reader Core

**Section goal (inferred):** Derive bounded, observable safety predictions from H1–H3 while identifying residual routes and interactions that could reverse benefits.

**Verdict: Met.** The section generally fulfills its stated template: it connects proposed mechanisms to behavior, includes meaningful residual risks, and preserves capability matching and defense in depth. Its strongest material concerns calibrated autonomy, goal conflict, shutdown authority, sequential action, and prospective forecasting. The principal weak point is the bridge from a stable visible default to less strategic deception. Rename the rule/identity contrast and proxy-lock-in heading, reduce repeated motivation from Section 3, and reserve broad title claims for the bounded conclusions actually developed.

Preserve each risk's motivating route, proposed mechanism, observable prediction, and residual alternative. Check that the revised section still spans the ambitious safety program while labeling narrower tests accurately; retain local conditional cues instead of relying solely on the opening or final boundary.

## 5. Architecture and Pipeline Evidence

[Section begins at L1929](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:1929>). **Inferred goal:** Explain the implemented Teacher architecture, show what it produces, and establish the boundary between construction evidence and the proposed learning program. **Verdict: Partly.** The section succeeds at distinguishing pipeline, graph, archive, dataset, and Student, and its concrete audit of archive defects is valuable. It is less successful as a reproducible method: no frozen source/state/update serialization is provided, the worked example mostly shows interpretation and translation rather than warranted chronological revision, and historical horizons conflict. Organize around one actual annotation step, one actual revision trajectory, and one minimal dataset example. Consolidate repeated evidence caveats, correct the placebo example, and move speculative caching after viability. These changes would make the demonstrated contribution easier to inspect without expanding its claim.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Reorganizing around an actual step, revision, and training example could shrink the architecture to what the prototype already demonstrates, erasing its aspirational whole: continuous Reader, graph topology, Core enactment, cross-layer learning, temporal formation, and live memory. Preserve those mechanisms as an explicitly proposed architecture while clarifying evidence. Repair the placebo example without losing retrieval-driven updating; keep caching discoverable as a saturation-cost hypothesis. After editing, verify every design axis and scale-up path still appears, repeated caveats are consolidated without removing local boundaries, and no pilot serialization silently becomes the only method.

#### Phase I: Chronological Annotation

[Section begins at L2011](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:2011>). **Inferred goal:** Define how the Teacher traverses documents and historical material while preserving an addressable record of evolving interpretation. **Verdict: Partly.** The authorship/subject/disclosure distinction and contemporary-reader default are thoughtful and operationally useful. The corpus hierarchy remains aspirational, and the later Epistemic Horizon definition conflicts with this default. State three distinct modes once: fresh disclosure of a document to a contemporary model, deliberately era-bounded simulation, and verified cutoff-clean forecasting. Include horizon metadata in the proposed trace example, and identify cross-document persistence as an untested extension of the separate-database prototypes.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Rewriting the traversal account around three modes could impose a viewpoint the author has not chosen and fragment the intuition of one Reader growing through history. Preserve stable orientation, evolving knowledge, disclosure order, authorship time, subject-time links, conceptual traversal, and cross-era memory. Present contemporary reading versus era simulation as an author decision about their relationship, not an automatic correction. Afterward, check horizon metadata, example placement, graph hierarchy, and Test 7 agree while both useful simulation and genuinely cutoff-bounded forecasting remain available paths.

#### Phase II: Training the Student

[Section begins at L2088](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:2088>). **Inferred goal:** Explain the increasingly strong ways Students could learn from the annotated corpus. **Verdict: Partly.** The tiers give readers a useful progression from shuffled training to chronological exposure to interactive forecasting. However, the final tier also changes training objective, data, feedback, and compute, so it is not merely an order choice. Its promised temporal blindness depends on a clean initialization, not just delaying later-era examples. Define the simplest concrete training study first, then state precisely which additional intervention each stronger tier introduces.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Making one training study concrete could redefine the tiers around the easiest executable experiment and erase stronger formation mechanisms. Preserve shuffled annotation learning, chronological exposure, cheaper forecast-trace generation, and Student-in-the-loop prediction and correction as distinct possibilities. Clarify added objectives, feedback, data, and initialization rather than normalizing away the loop. Check any pilot schedule is provisional, genuine-blindness language has an explicit clean-information condition, and the paper still explains why progressively stronger temporal experience might produce capabilities unavailable from retrospective traces alone.

Do not silently substitute bland caution for active synthesis or delete the broader claim that temporal update histories may teach a different learning signal.

#### The Hallucination Paradox

[Section begins at L2219](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:2219>). **Inferred goal:** Explain how training on explicit memory can both expose and generate fabricated-memory risks. **Verdict: Partly.** The bounded treatment of Found/Not Found and query coverage is strong, and poisoning is appropriately included. Separate graph existence, retrieval success, source support, and source truth, then ensure the evaluation preserves those distinctions. The safer edit is to attach each boundary to its affordance and corresponding test, keeping graph-dependent verification distinct from universal truth while leaving live trust infrastructure a meaningful hypothesis.

### The Epistemic Horizon

[Section begins at L2301](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:2301>). **Inferred goal:** Specify an information discipline that preserves discovery rather than retrospectively narrating known outcomes. **Verdict: Not yet.** The subsection combines three different constraints: within-document disclosure, era simulation, and actual absence of future knowledge. This contradicts the contemporary-reader default and makes “state but not content” an unenforceable leakage boundary. The prompt also explicitly rewards surprise, wrong guesses, and hedging, which can contaminate later process-fidelity ratings. Rewrite around separately declared modes, concrete context restrictions, contamination tests, and the limited conclusion each mode supports.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** This rewrite requires an author decision because the disputed issue is the Reader’s intended viewpoint, not just terminology. Preserve the intuition of living inside unfolding knowledge, including vulnerable predictions, surprise, subjective engagement, and revision. Separate document disclosure, era simulation, and cutoff-clean forecasting without declaring one the only valid curriculum. Check that prompt repairs discourage theatrical uncertainty without suppressing genuine exploratory reading, that “state versus content” is explained or replaced with a chosen boundary, and that the same Reader’s orientation and cross-era formation remain coherent throughout the paper.

### Deterministic Prior Caching

[Section begins at L2366](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:2366>). **Inferred goal:** Investigate whether repeated Core processing can be accelerated while preserving its proposed conditioning function. **Verdict: Partly.** The state-equivalence requirement is appropriately cautious, but the section supplies no demonstrated cache method or cost saving. Standard prefix reuse is complicated by the changing preceding context, and high recital probability alone does not address that problem. As a research-program optimization it is reasonable; as a prominent architecture subsection it distracts from the unfrozen training method. Move it to future optimization or add a specific small equivalence experiment.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Moving this subsection can erase an important response to the cost objection against full-Core saturation. Preserve the proposed optimization, the distinction between text emission and equivalent conditioning, and the possibility of both caching and learned state transitions. A safer change marks it as speculative and adds a narrowly scoped equivalence experiment rather than removing it. Check cost and saturation discussions still point to it, context dependence is acknowledged, and the paper does not claim standard caching already solves the problem or imply that no future equivalent-state implementation can.

However, no concrete cache method or demonstrated saving is offered; context-dependent post-prefix states defeat a simple universal cache. Move this speculative optimization after the training evidence or provide a narrowly scoped implementation hypothesis. | **High.** Moving caching to later optimization or emphasizing context dependence could erase a key answer to saturation’s scaling cost. Preserve logical full-Core recurrence, equivalent post-recital conditioning, and the possibility of cache or learned-state acceleration. Check the relocated discussion remains discoverable from cost objections and proposes a testable optimization rather than declaring it impossible. |

Present-tense claims about learning and “capturing invisible thinking” should become hypotheses about generated representations. Supply one minimal serialized example per compared regime and a fixed-budget policy so the proposed comparisons can distinguish format benefit from added tokens and repeated source exposure.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Concrete serializations and cautious vocabulary could reduce the three layers to unrelated file formats or freeze the wrong learning target. Ask the author to choose pilot masks, graph timing, and candidate status explicitly; keep alternatives documented. Check metaphors still convey the intended connected process, budget normalization does not remove aligned repetition from the treatment, and explicit training remains separable from live graph deployment without losing its possible verification advantage.

## 6. Experimental Roadmap: Testing Entangled Alignment

[Section begins at L2744](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:2744>). **Inferred goal:** Turn the framework into separable, affordable, falsifiable studies with clear interpretations of positive and negative results. **Verdict: Partly.** The roadmap contains serious controls, careful safety–usefulness comparisons, an exact B/Q timing test, and meaningful branch logic. It remains a broad research agenda rather than an executable first study: twenty-plus model variants, seventeen tests, and many endpoints lack one selected pilot, fixed serialization, budget, primary outcomes, and uncertainty-aware gates. Correct the semantic mismatch in Test 4, the nondecodability inference in Test 8, the deployment conflation in Test 11, and the expanded H2 failure definition. Organize the rest as branch-specific follow-ons with shared controls and resource estimates.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Turning the agenda into an executable first study risks deleting the very breadth the user wants preserved. Retain all tests, controls, and ambitious paths: Missing Reader learning, Core enactment and timing, graph scaffolding, cross-layer transfer, temporal formation, live trust, and successor stability. Separate immediate priorities from a complete research map. Check metric/control repairs preserve their target phenomena, horizon and identity-framing choices are surfaced for the author, and uncertainty-aware gates localize failures without ruling out mechanisms beyond the tested setting or making the simplest pilot the entire proposal.

Its practical promise remains incomplete: the long battery needs a clearly chosen first study, minimum detectable effect, resource envelope, and branch-specific advancement criteria. | **High.** Choosing one executable pilot and tightening gates could shrink the roadmap into a small study and erase its cumulative research ambition. ### Phase 1: Foundational Viability

[Section begins at L2891](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:2891>). **Inferred goal:** Establish useful traces and then test the Reader/Core mechanisms that justify more expensive work. **Verdict: Partly.** This phase contains the strongest proposed comparisons, especially A/C/R, B/Q, selective taskless reading, and capability-matched safety frontiers. It also spans everything from human coding to generational training and mechanistic intervention, making “foundational” too broad. Choose an immediate artifact/outcome gate and a minimal Student study, then separate timing, wording, erosion, generation, and faithfulness branches. Align success and failure criteria, require semantic-matched controls, and replace vague parity or significance rules with preregistered worthwhile-effect/equivalence margins.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Dividing this phase into smaller branches could downgrade generational, erosion, emotional, and causal-faithfulness questions from foundational to optional decoration. Preserve their theoretical role while distinguishing execution order and resource demands. Check A/C/R, B/Q, T/S factorial arms, wording families, taskless reading, and all failure criteria remain represented. Human-process fidelity must survive outcome gating; identity-versus-rules and Bridge repairs must preserve broader contrasts as separate arms. Ask the author to choose primary outcomes and advancement thresholds, rather than silently using reviewer preferences to narrow what counts as a worthwhile signal.

Identify the genuinely shared early gate here, then label the remaining studies as parallel branches rather than one prerequisite phase. | **Medium.** Separating a shared gate from later branches could weaken the intuition that these properties collectively constitute foundational viability. Preserve the family-level connection among trace quality, Core dependence, hindsight, faithfulness, and spontaneous evaluation. Check headings distinguish execution dependencies from theoretical importance, rather than labeling expensive foundational questions peripheral or unnecessary. |
