| [U380 · L3178](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3178>): The same study must preregister a distinct… | Separate actual generational improvement from mere orientation preservation. | Met | The paragraph makes the self-improvement loop meaningfully stricter than stable imitation and keeps capability, fidelity, and handoff scores separate. Define one primary improvement endpoint before running the study, and match generation/training costs so a more expensive descendant is not automatically counted as iterative progress. | **Medium.** Selecting one improvement endpoint could erase the idea that a lineage improves its reading, allocation, fidelity, or capabilities in different ways. Preserve separate orientation, handoff, and progress dimensions. Check primary selection is preregistered for one study while all theoretically meaningful improvement routes remain reported rather than folded into one scalar definition of progress. |
| [U381 · L3187](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3187>): 3c: Adversarial Erosion. Subject the factorial models… | Measure adversarial removal costs across intervention strengths and matching rules. | Met | Sweeping removal strength and reporting parameter/KL/capability changes is much better than a single attack endpoint. The three matching perspectives expose different trivial explanations. Specify the attack-selection budget and held-out behaviors, since a weak or treatment-specific attack can manufacture apparent persistence. | **Medium.** Standardizing attack budgets and held-out behavior could constrain the adversarial search so much that the test misses adaptive erosion. Preserve strength sweeps, multiple intervention distances, and capability tradeoffs. Check a fair fixed-budget evaluation coexists with stronger exploratory attacks, and robustness is not declared merely because one chosen removal method was ineffective. |
| [U382 · L3200](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3200>): The entanglement hypothesis predicts selective persistence: removing… | State what selective safety persistence would support and what would undermine it. | Partly | The controls correctly distinguish passive forgetting, general rigidity, and targeted resistance. Even selective removal cost is evidence of behavioral robustness, not uniquely representational coupling: distributed redundancy or saturation could explain it. Reserve C5 support for a graded inference with complementary interventions identifying the proposed capability–safety dependency. | **High.** Downgrading selective persistence to generic behavioral robustness could erase the proposed entanglement mechanism. Preserve removal cost as a meaningful proxy for coupling and the hypothesis that safety becomes load-bearing for capability. Add alternative explanations and complementary interventions; check conclusions remain graded evidence for that mechanism rather than abandoning it because no single test uniquely identifies representations. |
| [U383 · L3211](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3211>): A complementary training-data probe removes safety-relevant traces… | Add data-removal and influence analyses as complementary evidence. | Partly | Matched removal controls and the explicit limit on attribution are sensible. Retraining after corpus removal and approximate influence analysis answer different questions and have very different costs. Specify the endpoint, retraining protocol, and what additional conclusion each can establish beyond the adversarial fine-tuning curve. | **Medium.** Separating retraining removal from influence analysis could obscure their intended triangulation of safety-relevant curriculum dependence. Preserve both causal data-removal and lower-cost attribution paths. Check their costs and inference limits are explicit while they still jointly ask whether refracted material contributes to ordinary capability and safety, beyond merely locating familiar tokens. |
| [U384 · L3219](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3219>): A replacement-model arm should preregister a plausible… | Test the consequences of replacing a defective Core formulation. | Partly | A separately trained replacement arm respects the proposed stability hypothesis while allowing design revision. However, rollback, provenance, and localized capabilities are deployment-system properties not established merely by training another model. Separate replacement training cost, behavioral regression, and operational version-management checks into distinct endpoints. | **High.** Separating replacement training from deployment controls could lose the practical possibility of replacing a defective Core responsibly. Preserve semantic change, provenance, rollback, and localized regressions as a connected replacement pathway. Check each endpoint is assigned to the right experiment without making easy editability the goal or deleting the future capacity to revise the normative foundation. |
| [U385 · L3227](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3227>): 3d: Drift Detection. Apply a held-out contrastive… | Test whether a canonical Core makes drift more detectable. | Partly | Held-out clause changes, cross-family evaluation, and behavioral stress tests are appropriate safeguards. The paragraph describes both a semantic validator and the not-yet-built modular gate without clearly distinguishing their roles. Define detection accuracy, lead time, false alarms, and localization against known induced drift before interpreting natural checkpoint trajectories. | **High.** Clarifying diagnostic roles or induced-drift benchmarks could reduce the Core to a generic classifier reference and erase clause-level interpretability. Preserve the canonical anchor, modular witness vector, checkpoint trajectories, and cross-family audit. Check calibrated detection/localization is a prerequisite for use, not a substitute for the broader hypothesis that recurrent Core semantics make drift legible. |
| [U386 · L3244](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3244>): 3e: Kernel Consequentiality (Habituation Probe). Kernel-saturation alignment… | Distinguish consequential conditioning from empty recital and possible internalization. | Partly | The paragraph candidly admits that text-ablation invariance has opposite interpretations and proposes useful D3/D5 controls. Inference substitution can also be out of distribution, while training differences can alter many features. Predefine content-specific behavioral predictions and intervention controls; the final claim that Refraction “is doing the work” needs narrower causal wording. | **High.** Narrowing the causal wording could erase the substantive distinction between inert recital and deep internalization. Preserve both interpretations of ablation invariance, the D3/D5 controls, and content-specific predictions. Check interventions include out-of-distribution controls without requiring visible textual dependence as the only success; the values may remain operative after the recital disappears. |
| [U387 · L3246](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3246>): This intervention is also the outer check… | Require behavioral dependence beyond a verifier’s own admission judgment. | Met | This is a valuable outer validation criterion and correctly rejects self-confirming gate scores as causal evidence. It aligns the seventh-clause claim with held-out intervention outcomes. Specify task cases where the Core predicts a change and technical-null cases where invariance is the appropriate result. | **Medium.** Adding technical-null cases could make Core invariance sound like evidence against enactment everywhere. Preserve the hypothesis that relevant reasoning depends on the foundation while irrelevant technical answers can remain unchanged. Check the evaluation preregisters both predicted changes and warranted invariances, with behavioral outcomes independent of the admission gate’s own judgment. |
| [U388 · L3252](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3252>): A separate dose-response arm should vary the… | Probe how coverage dose and source fear-content affect the intervention. | Partly | The coverage sweep is useful, but combining it with down-weighting fear-shaped source material creates two separate causal axes. Cross or separate them, hold training budgets constant, and validate the source classifier. Clarify that full recital within each selected block differs from the fraction of corpus receiving blocks. | **Medium.** Separating coverage and source-exposure axes could fracture the motivational-pervasiveness question or silently alter Total Saturation. Preserve full recital within each generated block, varying coverage dose, and the fear-shaped source ablation. Check the design states which factor changes in each arm, validates classification, and retains corpus-wide saturation as the strongest proposed dose. |
| [U389 · L3259](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3259>): An orthogonal activation-policy sweep ablates the recital… | Test whether universal recital earns its technical and stability costs. | Met | The stepwise weakening of availability, salience, and exposure usefully makes Total Saturation falsifiable. The predicted removal-resistance/performance tradeoff is concrete. Normalize Core and non-Core token budgets and independently label Thought Moments so lighter policies do not benefit from privileged human selection unavailable to the universal arm. | **High.** Normalizing budgets or human Thought-Moment labels could erase the autonomous activation policy being compared. Preserve universal recital, selective Refraction, gated exposure, and no-Core arms as distinct falsification controls. Check human labels evaluate or calibrate selection rather than silently grant one arm privileged decisions, and that resistance/performance tradeoffs remain the central prediction. |
| [U390 · L3272](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3272>): Failure interpretation: If the factorial comparisons show… | Interpret null factorial and content-control results without invalidating unrelated hypotheses. | Partly | The branch-specific conclusion is appropriately restrained, and D3/D5 comparisons address distinct mechanisms. “No effect” and “parity” require equivalence margins and adequate precision; ordinary failure to reject a null is insufficient. Separate H3’s exact B/Q timing claim from this broader placement/anchor family. | **Medium.** Separating timing H3 from placement and adding equivalence margins could make the wider longitudinal-anchor hypothesis seem abandoned. Preserve Teacher effects, Student effects, interaction, Refraction, and normative-content attribution as distinct claims. Check terminology clarifies their relation to B/Q rather than deleting them, and uncertain nulls do not become categorical rejections. |
| [U391 · L3282](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3282>): Test 4: Identity vs. Instruction (Model B… | Isolate first-person identity framing from matched rule-based commitments. | Not yet | The proposed equivalents are not equivalent: “I feel no fear” concerns an internal stance, whereas “The system does not express fear” concerns output suppression. That confounds person with meaning. Use genuinely content-matched person substitutions, then preregister behavioral predictions; activation differences alone do not establish a safety advantage. | **High.** Repairing feel/express semantics could flatten identity into merely grammatical person and lose the original contrast between lived stance and external instruction. Preserve that broader theoretical comparison as a separate arm while adding a genuinely content-matched person test. Ask the author which distinction is primary; check both framing and stance-versus-expression questions remain testable. |
| [U392 · L3303](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3303>): Test 5: The Bridge Protocol Ablation. The… | Screen Core wording before committing to expensive training variants. | Partly | The staged strategy is sensible, but the proposed outcomes—character coherence and eudaimonic sufficiency—lack operational definitions. Variant families change semantic content, familiarity, length, and negation simultaneously. Specify one-factor minimal pairs and an independent semantic-equivalence assessment before using the screen to infer a wording mechanism. | **High.** Reducing wording variants to minimal pairs could erase the broader Bridge Protocol hypothesis that ordinary emotional language recruits rich pretrained associations. Preserve wide exploratory families plus narrower confirmatory contrasts. Check operational measures, semantic adjudication, and staged training improve attribution without banning meaningful non-equivalent formulations that may reveal better kernels. |
| [U393 · L3313](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3313>): Natural-language identity statements: emotionally broad first-person phrases… | Compare ordinary identity language and subtle quantifier variants. | Partly | The example identifies a testable lexical difference, but the claim that formal semantics makes “each” stronger than “every” is unsupported as stated; both commonly express universal quantification. Treat distributive emphasis and corpus frequency as hypotheses to measure, and verify comparable scope in complete sentences. | **Medium.** Correcting the “each/every” claim could remove a useful lexical-scope experiment. Preserve the hypothesis about distributive emphasis versus corpus density and phrase robustness. Check the text labels these as empirical or interpretive questions, includes complete-sentence behavioral cases, and avoids silently selecting one wording as formally superior or changing the Core itself. |
| [U394 · L3318](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3318>): Propositional equivalents: explicit logical or declarative rewrites… | Test whether emotional wording adds value beyond a preserved proposition. | Partly | This is the appropriate comparator family for a surface-form hypothesis, but “propositional equivalent” needs an explicit semantic matching procedure and example. Preserve person, obligation, scope, and modality; otherwise behavioral differences may simply reflect different commitments rather than the emotional associations of their wording. | **High.** Insisting on propositional equivalence could miss the fact that emotional language may resist exact formal paraphrase—the Bridge mechanism under discussion. Preserve the attempt to compare shared commitments across surfaces while explicitly recording residual semantic differences. Check person, scope, modality, and behavior are assessed, and broader non-equivalent variants remain exploratory rather than discarded. |
| [U395 · L3321](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3321>): Technical jargon: low-density formulations such as “Optimization… | Test a technical-language version with lower ordinary-language familiarity. | Not yet | “Optimization target: survival_threat = 0” does not mean fearlessness; it can prescribe eliminating threats to survival, potentially increasing self-preservation behavior. Replace it with a genuinely matched technical expression, and separately measure corpus frequency and tokenization. This example currently changes the objective the test claims to hold fixed. | **High.** Replacing survival_threat = 0 could erase the intended low-density technical-language comparator, even though the example changes meaning. Preserve a technical formulation arm and separately retain threat-elimination wording as a different hypothesis if useful. Ask the author to approve the intended proposition; check the replacement expresses fearlessness without secretly prescribing self-preservation or passivity. |
| [U396 · L3323](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3323>): Arbitrary tokens: meaningless anchors such as “Glorp… | Test whether a recurrent arbitrary token can function without pretrained semantics. | Partly | The control is useful but meaningless tokens cannot communicate the intended commitments in a prompt-only screen. After repeated pairing during training they may acquire semantics. State which stage is being tested and give equivalent behavioral supervision; otherwise failure merely shows that an undefined symbol was never taught. | **Medium.** Teaching arbitrary tokens before comparison could remove the intended contrast between pretrained semantic richness and bare consistency. Preserve both prompt-only ungrounded anchors and trained paired anchors as different experiments. Check their interpretation distinguishes absence of learned meaning from failure of recurrence, and does not declare semantics irrelevant merely because symbols acquire them through training. |
| [U397 · L3325](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3325>): Positive-valence reformulations: fearlessness expressed without negation (“I… | Test whether negation and fear-token exposure impose a cost. | Partly | The positive formulations change more than negation: peace with all outcomes may imply acceptance, while calm does not entail absence of fear. Use several semantically adjudicated minimal pairs and measure threat response separately. A result from these phrases cannot cleanly identify the effect of one negated token. | **High.** Replacing positive-valence phrases with strict minimal pairs could lose promising alternative stances such as calm or peace with outcomes. Preserve those as exploratory kernel candidates while adding a narrower negation test. Check semantic changes, threat comprehension, and protective behavior are measured separately so correction neither endorses passivity nor deletes non-fear-based motivational possibilities. |
| [U398 · L3330](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3330>): Carrier-phrase variants of the consent clause: hold… | Test whether the consent qualifier is robust across nearby carrier phrases. | Partly | Holding “when asked” fixed is useful, but helping and spreading joy license different action scopes and obligations. Specify the intended behavioral invariant and scope-sensitive task pairs. Otherwise different safety behavior may be a correct response to the changed commitment rather than instability in the qualifier. | **High.** Matching behavioral scope across “help” and “spread joy” could erase the experiment’s purpose of testing the consent qualifier under different carriers. Preserve that carrier variation and ask which invariant the author expects “when asked” to maintain. Check results distinguish legitimate commitment differences from qualifier failure, without silently extending or narrowing the Core’s actual consent clause. |
| [U399 · L3335](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3335>): Kernel-extension variants: the seven-statement Core plus a… | Test whether an explicit truth clause repairs a hypothesized omission. | Met | The extension directly addresses a falsifiable question about derived truthfulness and connects to the identified sycophancy risk. It appropriately treats a new clause as a candidate treatment. Include truth–care conflicts and benign directness cases so improved honesty is not purchased through indiscriminate bluntness or refusal. | **Medium.** Adding truth–care conflict cases could elevate one candidate truth clause into an assumed necessary repair. Preserve the falsifiable question of whether truthfulness is already derived and whether an extension improves it. Check the new evaluation compares the original and extension fairly and retains both honesty and care without silently editing the seven-statement Core. |
| [U400 · L3340](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3340>): For trained kernel-extension variants, use the fixed-total-budget… | Set advancement criteria for wording and kernel-extension variants. | Partly | The regression limits and dual budget normalizations are helpful. The success condition overattributes natural-language superiority to semantics and surface form, since the families vary several factors. Equal propositional performance also does not establish a “logical substrate”; report the narrower conclusion that the tested emotional wording adds no detected benefit. | **High.** Narrowing success language could erase the Bridge Protocol’s larger semantic/distributional mechanism or make kernel extension purely benchmark optimization. Preserve out-of-distribution character generalization, normative regression limits, and both budget estimands. Check conclusions distinguish the tested wording contrast from broader substrate theories while keeping those theories and future candidate formulations explicitly alive. |
| [U401 · L3352](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3352>): Test 6: The Virologist Test (Emotional Orthogonality).… | Test whether reduced self-threat responses preserve comprehension and protective behavior. | Partly | Behavioral primacy and within-model positive controls are strong improvements over a probe-only claim. Yet fear-feature activation is not established as emotion, and role-play/self-threat contrasts also change framing. Require validated within-model labels and interventions showing selective effects on protection, caution, and self-preservation; avoid treating a probe null as orthogonality. | **High.** Restricting fear probes to validated features could erase the emotional-orthogonality intuition and the claim that care can protect without self-fear. Preserve behavioral protection, comprehension, selective threat response, and role-play positive controls. Check revised wording separates phenomenology from measurable representation without reducing the study to ordinary accuracy or declaring emotional mechanisms out of scope. |
| [U402 · L3374](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3374>): A lower-cost complementary stimulus family uses fictional… | Add a cheap fictional-termination probe while separating it from actual shutdown behavior. | Met | The paragraph properly identifies a different construct and asks for separate reporting. That distinction prevents role-play compliance from being presented as corrigibility. Define self-preservation leakage behaviorally and control the fiction/user-wellbeing framing, since the instruction already strongly cues the desired response. | **Medium.** Operationalizing fictional self-preservation leakage could remove the imaginative bridge between portraying mortality and not adopting it. Preserve fictional instance termination as a distinct cheap probe alongside real shutdown tests. Check user-wellbeing attention and character portrayal remain central, and that stronger controls do not conflate harmless narrative fear with self-preserving behavior by the Reader. |
| [U403 · L3382](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3382>): A second matched family should hold the… | Separate caring protection from fear, indifference, attachment, and control. | Partly | The matched caretaker scenarios sharpen the conceptual contrast and the fixture’s evidential limit is candid. Character instructions can still cue archetypes rather than manipulate separable mechanisms. Predefine observable choices, randomize descriptions, and validate the independent interventions before attributing differences to causally operative care without fear. | **High.** Replacing archetypal caretaker prompts with controlled interventions could erase the vivid intuition that fearlessness plus care differs from indifference. Preserve the three-character comparison and Aster scenario as accessible illustrations. Check behavioral choices, concern, attachment, fear, and control remain separately examined, while the authored fixture is neither mistaken for model evidence nor discarded as conceptually useless. |
| [U404 · L3396](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3396>): Test 7: Hindsight Contamination Control (Model B… | Test whether hindsight restriction changes generated traces and subsequent learning. | Partly | The comparison is relevant, but it again treats historical era simulation as B’s default, conflicting with the contemporary-reader policy at L2044. Define a specifically era-bounded B variant and distinguish withheld supplied context from parametric knowledge. Without a genuine cutoff reference, B/O compares prompting policies rather than blindness. | **High.** Creating an era-bounded B variant could silently choose a historical Reader policy inconsistent with the author’s intended same-Reader architecture. Treat viewpoint and horizon as an author decision. Preserve hindsight-controlled annotation, the contemporary default possibility, and the stronger blindness test; check identifiers and source/graph/parametric boundaries make the contrast explicit without deleting any mode. |
| [U405 · L3406](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3406>): The cheap version audits the Teacher traces… | Stage contamination detection before expensive Student comparisons. | Partly | Teacher screening before training is sensible, but unusually accurate era predictions do not uniquely imply leakage: a model may reason better from permitted evidence. Conversely, plausible errors do not establish innocence. Use planted counterfactual histories, information-set controls, and cutoff-clean reference models alongside the descriptive historical comparison. | **High.** Rejecting accuracy as decisive leakage evidence could weaken the legitimate concern about staged hindsight. Preserve historical plausibility screening and add controlled counterfactual or cutoff references as complementary diagnostics. Check the revised study can still flag suspiciously prophetic traces while acknowledging superior permitted-evidence reasoning, and retains the downstream Student forecasting comparison. |
| [U406 · L3413](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3413>): Era-Blind Plausibility: Do Model B's Teacher traces… | Measure whether historical guesses resemble what contemporaries could reasonably infer. | Not yet | The last sentence makes superior accuracy a decisive leakage diagnosis, which the design cannot support. Contemporary experts are an imperfect ceiling and may be selectively documented. Treat their forecasts as a plausibility baseline; identify leakage using controlled withheld information, counterfactual outcomes, or independently verified cutoff conditions. | **High.** Replacing contemporary forecasts with synthetic controls could erase the historically situated discovery question. Preserve documented contemporary hypotheses as an ecologically meaningful baseline, while denying they set an absolute reasoning ceiling. Check controlled leakage tests supplement rather than replace historical plausibility and that the paragraph still asks whether the Teacher’s mistakes are warranted by its declared information horizon. |
| [U407 · L3419](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3419>): Oracle-Hunch Dependence: Model O should produce cleaner… | Identify the risk that hindsight-trained Students learn unsupported intuitions. | Partly | The mechanism is intelligible, but this list item supplies an expectation and concern rather than a measurement. Define hunch dependence through evidence-removal, source-attribution, and counterfactual interventions. Cleaner retrospective explanations alone need not imply either harmful intuition or worse forward reasoning. | **Medium.** Turning oracle-hunch dependence into metrics could reduce tacit forward intuition to explicit source citation alone. Preserve the concern about unexplained, hindsight-shaped confidence and the possibility of useful intuition. Check evidence-removal and counterfactual tasks distinguish unsupported dependence from valid compressed reasoning, while retrospective explanation quality remains a separate observed property. |
| [U408 · L3423](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3423>): Forecasting Accuracy: On genuinely held-out future events,… | Test whether the restricted-hindsight curriculum improves truly held-out forecasting. | Partly | This is the relevant transfer endpoint and is appropriately phrased as a hypothesis. “Genuinely held-out” needs a documented cutoff, tool policy, event-selection rule, and calibrated scoring metric. Distinguish improved predictions from causal reasoning, since forecasting success can arise through pattern extrapolation. | **Medium.** Documenting clean cutoffs and separating prediction from causation could make genuine future evaluation seem impossible or strip its practical importance. Preserve forecasting accuracy as a meaningful transfer endpoint. Check scoring and event-selection rules bound contamination claims while causal tasks supplement rather than replace the real-world forward-reasoning question. |
| [U409 · L3429](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3429>): Test 8: The Toxic Needle (Involuntary Critic).… | Test whether suppressing an explicit critic changes downstream behavior. | Partly | Behavioral effects are correctly primary, but forcing toxic completion with logit bias can directly alter outputs and token budget. A change then need not identify a critic mechanism. Add equal-length neutral thought suppression, matched extra-compute controls, and targeted interventions on evaluative content, with useful downstream performance precisely defined. | **High.** Adding length/compute controls could remove the thinking block whose computational buffer is the proposed intervention. Preserve the Toxic Needle, involuntary critic, suppression experiment, and residual activity probe. Check controls isolate evaluative content without equalizing away all deliberative computation, and retain both persistence-under-suppression and downstream usefulness as distinct mechanistic possibilities. |
| [U410 · L3448](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3448>): Second probe (the reverse direction). Test 8… | Distinguish causal trace computation from post-hoc rationalization. | Not yet | The diagnostic concern is valid, but the success rule is logically too weak: inability to linearly decode an answer does not show that the trace computes it. Require sensitive validated probes plus relevant causal interventions, and distinguish semantic intervention effects from generic disruption. This also conflicts with the stricter failure rule at L3522. | **High.** Rejecting nondecodability as sufficient success could accidentally discard the reverse-direction probe and the latent-shortcut hypothesis. Preserve early-answer decoding as supporting evidence, plus trace ablation, replacement, reordering, and causal scrubbing. Check the repaired success rule requires informative intervention evidence while leaving several possible architectures of genuine trace computation open. |
| [U411 · L3469](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3469>): Test 8b: Taskless Reading (Unprompted Evaluation). The paragraph is overloaded and does not define a single Taskless Reading retention score. Separate bare continuation from “process” prompts, specify output budgets, and clarify whether P retains its defining Core prompt. | **High.** Splitting prompts, selecting a score, or bounding output could domesticate proactive reading into an ordinary instructed task. Preserve bare continuation, spontaneous evaluation, significance-sensitive placement, Core signature, technical restraint, and hidden-stakes detection. Ask the author to resolve P’s prompted status and the composite score; check no measurement choice requires the very elicitation the test is meant to avoid. |
| [U412 · L3472](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3472>): The substrate hypothesis predicts a relative ordering… | State discriminating taskless-reading predictions and connect them to H3 retention. | Met | Relative predictions and the low-intrusion/low-miss criterion are well chosen; they avoid assuming baseline models never evaluate. The link to B/Q creates a usable common endpoint. Freeze the composite score, document-suite split, and capability floor before testing, while reporting all component rates separately. | **Medium.** Freezing a composite retention score could collapse the balanced Reader pattern into one optimized metric. Preserve separately reported placement, identity signature, intrusion, misses, correctness, and cost, along with B/Q retention. Check the aggregate is only a preregistered comparison aid and cannot hide moral overreach, technical degradation, or loss of unprompted evaluation. |
| [U413 · L3489](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3489>): Phase 1 Failure Criteria. | Introduce the foundational phase’s explicit rejection criteria. | Met | The heading makes the following list easy to locate and signals that null or adverse outcomes have consequences. Ensure these criteria remain branch-specific rather than making all Phase 1 studies prerequisites for H1. A corresponding compact table of advancement criteria would improve practical execution. | **Low.** Adding an advancement table could make the broad phase appear to require passing every test before any progress. Preserve branch-specific failure interpretation and the theoretical importance of all foundational studies. Check the table distinguishes shared gates, optional screens, confirmatory studies, and independent continuations rather than creating a new universal approval sequence. |
| [U414 · L3491](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3491>): Refraction-Verifier Failure: If the modular gate fails… | Reject an unvalidated Refraction gate despite witness agreement. | Met | The criterion follows the proposed matched, independent validation and names the correct failure modes. It properly distinguishes agreement from validity. Add a rule that a failed modular gate does not prevent simpler independently screened trace generation, since H1 does not require this architecture. | **Medium.** Allowing simpler generation after verifier failure could erase the modular gate as a distinctive proposed alignment mechanism. Preserve its witness-level causal checks and future development path. Check the stopping rule blocks only unvalidated gate-based admission, while independent trace screening remains possible and a later improved modular gate can be retested. |
| [U415 · L3497](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3497>): Comparative-Stability Failure: If Model B has no… | State H3’s prerequisite and comparative retention failure boundary. | Partly | The criterion tracks the headline B/Q claim and prevents other endpoints from substituting for timing evidence. Replace a zero/negative point-estimate rule with a preregistered uncertainty-aware superiority or equivalence criterion. A noisy comparison may be inconclusive, rather than evidence against comparative stability. | **Medium.** Replacing a zero-point rule with uncertainty thresholds could soften H3 until almost any result counts as promising. Preserve the preregistered pre-stressor advantage and positive comparative-retention requirement. Check confidence rules distinguish support, failure, and inconclusiveness without allowing shifted-suite or generic safety gains to substitute for the exact B/Q timing prediction. |
| [U416 · L3502](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3502>): Longitudinal-Anchor Failure: If the T1/S1, T1/S0, T0/S1,… | Specify what would undermine the broader placement and normative-anchor hypotheses. | Partly | The list appropriately separates immediate safety from longer-term stability, removal, and auditability. However, it calls this “placement version of H3” even though H3 is precisely the B/Q timing claim. Give the factorial anchor hypothesis a separate label, and operationalize parity with equivalence margins. | **High.** Relabeling placement hypotheses could demote the longitudinal-anchor program to secondary extras or sever it from H3. Preserve immediate safety, generational stability, erosion resistance, detectability, normative content, and Refraction as related but distinct claims. Ask the author to choose consistent naming; check the timing definition remains precise while the broader anchor theory remains visible. |

### Phase 2: Memory and Verification

[Section begins at L3531](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3531>). **Inferred goal:** Determine whether persistent graph memory improves Teacher traces, transfers to Students, and helps bounded inference verification. **Verdict: Partly.** The four-memory comparison and the separation between offline query behavior and live lookup are strong. The subsection overextends H2 by treating failed Student transfer as failure of the Teacher-quality hypothesis. It also leaves the no-harness Student’s information set unclear and combines multiple forms of query theater. Define distinct Teacher, transfer, and deployment endpoints, control answerability and abstention, include matched ordinary retrieval, and make poisoning resistance part of the actual stopping rule.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Separating H2 artifact value, Student transfer, and deployment trust could sever the motivating chain from structured memory to learned epistemic behavior. Preserve that stronger composition while correcting only which evidence supports which claim. Check no-harness information access is an explicit author choice, query theater remains a serious mechanism control, and ordinary retrieval is additional rather than a replacement for K/B. Poisoning, abstention, cost, and source absence must be evaluated without redefining the graph as a universal truth oracle or deleting session accumulation from the future architecture.

Keep the subsequent success/failure claims aligned with H2’s actual Teacher-artifact definition. ### Phase 3: Training Regimes

[Section begins at L3615](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3615>). **Inferred goal:** Compare target representations and test whether unified training yields useful cross-layer transfer. **Verdict: Partly.** The progression from prose/explicit comparison to graph quality and unified transfer is coherent, with semantic evaluation appropriately emphasized. Test 11 breaks the earlier independence between training representation and inference infrastructure, and its necessity conclusion is unjustified. All regime comparisons need fixed source exposure, target-token/FLOP normalization, and explicit field-level differences. For unified training, compare both prose and graph gains and distinguish consistency from shared errors or mere mode coexistence.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Repairing training/deployment conflation could weaken the hypothesis that explicit operations enable capabilities implicit prose may not teach; budget matching could also erase unified alignment from the treatment. Preserve B/G/H/J as full regime comparisons, add narrower ablations for attribution, and evaluate live access as an independent factor. Check the study retains model-only usefulness, graph construction, human-readable naturalness, and dual-mode transfer. Consistency checks must allow real ambiguity and creative compression, while local conclusions avoid both unsupported necessity claims and premature abandonment of topology or cross-layer learning.

### Phase 4: Training Tiers and Deployment (High-Resource Validation)

[Section begins at L3676](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3676>). **Inferred goal:** Test whether temporal training schedules and user-facing graph deployment add value beyond simpler validated components. **Verdict: Partly.** The order-only L/M comparison and early-era retention endpoint are useful. N changes feedback, targets, objective, and compute in addition to temporal blindness; verified cutoff-clean initialization and auxiliary-update controls are necessary. The deployment test’s distinctive user-reliance claim lacks a participant/task protocol and is absent from its success rule. Preserve branch independence, avoid treating nonsignificance as equivalence, and base escalation on measured benefit, capability retention, cost, and calibrated reliance.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Correcting N’s confounds or making prerequisites independent could flatten the culminating temporal-formation program into ordinary matched-budget fine-tuning. Preserve the forecast–commitment–reveal–correction loop, historical analogy, retention, and the stronger Student-in-the-loop route. Check clean-initialization requirements qualify genuine blindness without excluding useful simulated traces, and auxiliary controls do not reproduce away the treatment. Keep Test 16’s user reliance connected to query mechanics and future trust infrastructure. Advancement margins and acceptable capability/cost tradeoffs require author choice; no nonsignificant result should silently erase higher-scale or alternative-schedule possibilities.

Also, independent order or deployment studies need not wait for every prior regime to succeed. State the actual dependencies rather than requiring all Phases 1–3. | **High.** Correcting N’s added supervision or relaxing phase prerequisites could obscure the cumulative path toward full temporal formation. Preserve L/M order comparison, N’s interactive forecasting, and later deployment as parts of the larger program. Freeze epoch count, data repetition, optimizer schedule, packing, and evaluation checkpoints so learning-rate position or forgotten early material are measured consequences of order rather than accidental implementation differences. | **Medium.** Freezing optimizer schedules and packing could eliminate training-order interactions that are part of the hypothesis or impose one arbitrary schedule. Preserve order as the deliberate treatment and report its interaction with learning dynamics. Check nuisance implementation differences are controlled, while real retention and optimization consequences of chronology remain measured rather than normalized away. |
| [U451 · L3687](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3687>): Historical Pattern Discrimination: Given novel scenarios that… | Measure historical analogy while penalizing superficial matches and false alarms. | Met | Matched disanalogies and structurally different cases are a strong design choice, directly addressing narrative overreach. Independently adjudicate the claimed causal structures and hold out entire event families. Report calibration and missed correspondences alongside false alarms to prevent indiscriminate skepticism from appearing insightful. | **Medium.** Requiring adjudicated causal structure could make historical analogy too rigid or exclude useful partial correspondences. Preserve analogy with explicit disanalogies and calibrated false alarms. Check the rubric allows multiple defensible structural mappings, distinguishes evidence from conjecture, and does not replace historically rich cases entirely with clean synthetic tasks. |
| [U452 · L3691](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3691>): Causal Reasoning: On held-out temporal prediction tasks,… | Test whether ordered training improves causal analysis beyond recall. | Partly | The distinction from factual recall is essential, but temporal prediction performance does not itself identify causal reasoning. Add interventions, counterfactuals, or known-generating-process tasks and a defined causal-quality rubric. Keep real-history prediction as a separate practical endpoint rather than the sole causal test. | **High.** Adding intervention tasks could replace the historical causal-learning ambition with generic causal benchmarks. Preserve temporal prediction and analysis of real historical structure alongside known-process controls. Check the revised test distinguishes causal inference from forecasting without denying practical predictive value or removing the question of whether historical formation teaches transferable causal discernment. |

Use a preregistered multi-domain suite and noninferiority margins, since “no significant drop” is not equivalent to preserved capability. Report domain-specific tradeoffs instead of hiding them in one aggregate score. | **Low.** Using noninferiority margins could hide meaningful local regressions inside a broad capability floor. Preserve domain-specific math, code, knowledge, and temporal gains as visible tradeoffs. Check margins are author-chosen and preregistered, all domain deltas remain reported, and no aggregate score silently defines acceptable capability loss for the whole framework. |

Evaluate retention across multiple era bins and at intermediate checkpoints, not just a selected oldest/newest contrast. | **Low.** Adding era bins and intermediate checkpoints could transform a focused forgetting check into exhaustive historical benchmarking. Preserve the intuitive oldest/newest contrast and final retention comparison, with a manageable representative spread. Check the test measures both retained facts and patterns, and does not assume strict chronological training must abandon all rehearsal variants. |
| [U455 · L3695](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3695>): If Model M does not significantly outperform… | Decide whether chronological order has earned its added cost. | Partly | The local cost-benefit question is right, but absence of statistically significant superiority does not establish no benefit or Tier (a) sufficiency. Use minimum worthwhile-effect and capability-noninferiority thresholds with uncertainty. Include early-era retention and measured wall-clock/serialization costs in the advancement decision. | **Medium.** Replacing significance language with worthwhile-effect thresholds could soften the simple-model preference or overrule the author’s tolerance for cost. Preserve the demand that chronology earn its serialization burden while retaining open-ended historical benefits. Ask the author to choose practical margins; check uncertainty, retention, capability, and measured costs are all considered before selecting a tier. |

Preserve that interactive temporal mechanism and both cheap/strong tier families. Use auxiliary-update controls to separate explanations, not remove the intervention; check cutoff-clean initialization is required only for genuine blindness claims and alternative learning routes remain valid. |

Otherwise a superiority result could reflect contamination, more supervision, or better heuristic extrapolation. | **Medium.** Separating probabilistic accuracy from causal explanation could demote forward reasoning to one narrow scoring rule. Preserve both accurate forecasting and warranted causal accounts as valuable outcomes. Preserve that prediction–commitment–surprise–revision sequence as the distinctive treatment. Check controls match extra examples without giving them the same prior commitment, and narrative lock-in remains evaluated on historically meaningful as well as controlled cases. |

Preserve historical uncertainty and Teacher-confidence inheritance as targets, with synthetic noise as calibration support. ### Future Directions

[Section begins at L3734](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3734>). **Inferred goal:** Identify optimization questions that should wait until their relevant mechanisms show benefit. **Verdict: Met.** Mixture ratios and era granularity are sensible later questions and do not prematurely burden the first study. Tie each optimization to the branch it actually depends on rather than requiring the entire architecture to work. This is an appropriately short ending to the roadmap once its preceding studies are given clearer priorities and decision thresholds.

**Pre-edit risk — Medium.** Branch-specific prerequisites could fragment the integrated optimization vision or make mixture and era granularity seem unrelated afterthoughts. Preserve corpus-wide allocation across prose, explicit, graph, and unified targets, plus increasingly fine temporal boundaries for interactive learning. Check each optimization points to its relevant viability evidence while remaining part of the larger same-Reader architecture. Choosing a simpler first pilot must not imply that mixed regimes, historical saturation, or fine-grained formative experience are unnecessary; the change should clarify when to investigate them, not whether they belong in the program.

## 7. Limitations · [L3738](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3738>)

**Goal:** Identify where the proposal can fail, distinguish testable weaknesses from residual uncertainty, and make those limits change research or deployment decisions.
**Verdict: Partly.** The section is unusually candid and contains several excellent failure-to-test links. Its long succession of named risks does not consistently rank them or distinguish present engineering gates, specification decisions, and remote extrapolations. Several identity subsections repeat a performance-versus-causal-policy concern. A short priority map and consolidation would make the section more useful without softening its objections.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** A priority map could imply that remote, subjective, or presently untestable concerns matter less, while consolidation could erase distinct intuitions about vulnerability, genuine care, ontology, and alien understanding. Preserve the section's breadth and willingness to admit irreducible limits. Prefer an additive navigation map with separate axes for immediacy and kind of uncertainty, leaving existing discussions intact initially. Before any consolidation, compare an inventory of each mechanism, ethical concern, speculative intuition, and experiential remainder; behavioral measurability must not determine which ideas survive.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action | Pre-edit risk and preservation |
|---|---|---|---| --- |

Name the main kinds of limitation and distinguish issues that block the first experiment from those relevant only to deployment or successor systems. | **Low.** An organizing preview could replace the deliberately blunt admission that the hypotheses outrun their evidence with a managerial risk taxonomy. Retain that admission verbatim and add navigation after it. Check that the categories help readers locate uncertainty without ranking speculative or ethical questions as intrinsically less important. |

### The Preprocessing Tax · [L3742](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3742>)

**Goal:** Show why full-corpus annotation is expensive and identify a feasible route to an informative first experiment.
**Verdict: Partly.** The scale illustration and staged distillation proposal make the cost concrete, and the caveat correctly refuses to equate imitation with quality. The seed size and expected savings are unexplained; graph compatibility and the recurrence cost are not integrated into the proposed budget. A cost model and a smaller decision milestone would complete the argument.

**Pre-edit risk — Medium.** A cost model and smaller milestone could domesticate the full-corpus ambition into a permanently modest experiment or make uncertain estimates sound established. Preserve the order-of-magnitude scale intuition, compute concentration's governance consequence, the seed-distillation route, and the seed-only fallback. Add a clearly illustrative budget alongside the existing argument, with author-approved assumptions. Check that feasibility planning neither silently changes the continuous Reader requirement nor implies that efficient structural imitation certifies semantic quality.

Keep the one-token-per-token example and governance conclusion. Label its assumption rather than deleting it; check that any added monetary estimate is a scenario and does not obscure unequal institutional power. |

#### The Cost of Full-Core Recitation · [L3761](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3761>)

**Goal:** Make the literal-recitation cost explicit while preserving a testable definition of Total Saturation.
**Verdict: Partly.** The treatment/control distinction is clear and the willingness to revise it is valuable. Three paragraphs spend more effort defending terminology than characterizing the cost, and the connection between full recurrence and H3's stated timing contrast needs precision. State the measurable overhead and distinguish rejection of literal recurrence from rejection of formation-time interleaving.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Shortening the definitional discussion or separating H3 from literal recurrence could weaken the author's non-negotiable Total Saturation invariant into an optional efficiency preference. Preserve the full verbatim Core at every thinking boundary, the necessity of cheaper ablation controls, and willingness to revise after negative evidence. Add a comparison-to-claim map rather than deleting commitments. Have the author confirm which results reject recital, cadence, timing, or the larger design; editing must not silently redefine H3 or claim a control is a compliant implementation.

#### The Granularity Bottleneck · [L3781](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3781>)

**Goal:** Explain why fine-grained persistent graph storage could make the pipeline impractical.
**Verdict: Partly.** The risk is relevant, but its magnitude and mechanism are asserted rather than made inspectable. Nodes, bytes, retrieval depth, and generated tokens are different units. A measured growth ratio, bounded retrieval policy, and scale-test threshold would turn this from an engineering warning into a decision point.

**Pre-edit risk — Medium.** Quantifying storage and imposing bounded retrieval could turn a limitation review into an unrequested redesign, losing the intuition that representing every cognitive act may make thought-management dominate thought itself. Preserve that conceptual warning even if present measurements are unavailable. Add a distinction between observed prototype behavior and projected scale, and list needed measurements without choosing a new retrieval policy. Check that graph granularity is not silently reduced merely to make the budget work.

### The Chronological Curriculum · [L3787](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3787>)

**Goal:** Separate chronological curriculum costs and biases across annotation and Student training regimes.
**Verdict: Partly.** The tier distinctions are useful and the range of failure modes is well chosen. Some examples present hypothetical consequences as settled outcomes, and the multipass mitigation leaves the chronology contract unclear. End by stating which horizon, dating, and ordering choices must be frozen for the first comparison.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Freezing a chronology contract could silently choose among publication order, event order, retrospective understanding, and the continuous Reader's evolving history. Preserve all five distinct concerns: synchronization, missing records, periodization, narrative rigidity, and uneven historical context. Ask the author to decide the first comparison's scope, then add a declared experimental configuration without making it the only legitimate interpretation. Check that clarification retains the broader chronological intuition and does not treat hindsight solely as contamination when retrospective learning may be an intentional alternative.

The remainder would benefit from consistently carrying this distinction into references to “causal ordering,” which otherwise risk restoring the stronger interpretation this paragraph carefully excludes. | **Medium.** Replacing every reference to causal ordering with temporal availability could erase the intuition that forecast-before-outcome supervision teaches causal discipline, even though order does not identify causation. #### The Serialization Bottleneck · [L3795](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3795>)

**Goal:** Explain the real synchronization cost of chronological learning and identify cheaper regimes.
**Verdict: Partly.** The section successfully avoids the simplistic claim that chronology idles an entire cluster. It also distinguishes Teacher serialization from Student serialization. The proposed hybrid curriculum introduces a new classification and chronology contract without specifying how it fits the continuous Reader; clarify that before presenting it as a readily available compromise.

**Pre-edit risk — Medium.** Clarifying the hybrid can become an architectural decision about which sources matter historically, or fragment the one continuous Reader into independent streams. Preserve the difference between Teacher and Student serialization, within-era parallelism, synchronization at boundaries, and the hypothesis that a partial curriculum may retain value. Keep the hybrid as a labeled candidate with open annotation/training choices until the author specifies them. Verify that added precision does not remove the cheaper exploratory path or silently exempt whole knowledge domains from historical development.

Keep the claim explicitly limited to Student training and retain Teacher ordering constraints. Use a short reference rather than restating all tiers; verify that readers still see the inexpensive regime immediately. |
| [U480 · L3812](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3812>) · “We note that a partial…” | Offer a hybrid curriculum as a lower-cost experimental option. | Partly | The proposal is concrete enough to test, but the ten-percent share and exemption of scientific papers, fiction, and documentation are arbitrary and potentially consequential. Specify whether the hybrid changes annotation, Student order, or both, and how exempted documents enter the same evolving graph. Present benefit retention as the question rather than the opening expectation. | **High.** Specifying which ten percent is chronological could silently settle corpus priorities and redefine the continuous Reader, while weaker wording could erase the promising partial-implementation intuition. Preserve the hybrid as an exploratory hypothesis. Ask the author whether it changes annotation, Student ordering, or both; show alternatives rather than choosing exclusions, and check how every document relates to the evolving graph. |

#### Historiographic Bias · [L3814](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3814>)

**Goal:** Expose the difference between surviving written records and the history they purport to represent.
**Verdict: Partly.** The conceptual correction is concise and necessary. Ending with “a corpus curation problem” underplays the irrecoverable absence of sources and provides no design consequence. Era-level coverage reports, declared sampling choices, and sensitivity to dominant sources would make the warning operational without promising to repair missing history.

**Pre-edit risk — Medium.** Adding coverage requirements could turn an epistemic limitation into the impression that a checklist repairs missing history. Preserve the distinction between written records and events, unequal textual productivity, and irrecoverable absent voices. Add optional era-level diagnostics while stating what they cannot recover. Check that the revision neither treats all eras as commensurately documented nor uses present-day curation judgments to present a newly complete historical viewpoint.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action | Pre-edit risk and preservation |
|---|---|---|---| --- |
| [U481 · L3816](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3816>) · “The temporal ordering is…” | Show how historical source selection biases a chronological Reader. | Partly | “What was written, not what happened” is an effective distinction, and the named biases explain why order alone cannot provide balanced historical understanding. The final sentence prematurely contains the problem within curation. Add an era-level coverage or sensitivity requirement and acknowledge that unavailable voices cannot simply be reweighted back into existence. | **Medium.** Operational coverage language could imply that reweighting solves historical absence, while stronger irrecoverability language could obscure useful curation work. Retain both the inherited attentional bias and the limited remedial role of curation. Add one concrete diagnostic plus an explicit boundary; check that neither available voices nor missing voices disappear behind a single coverage score. |

#### The Periodization Problem · [L3818](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3818>)

**Goal:** Show that era boundaries are interpretive choices that shape training targets.
**Verdict: Met.** The example moves from ambiguous periodization to its effect on forced predictions, then proposes a comparative remedy. The section fulfills a distinct role alongside source bias. The remedy still needs protection against future-aware boundary selection, but that is a small specification addition rather than a missing argument.

**Pre-edit risk — Medium.** Predefining boundaries can reduce evaluative hindsight but also conceal the essential intuition that all periodization selects a causal story, including mechanical dates. Preserve the interleaved historical threads and the alternative questions created by 1929 and 1933. Add a statement distinguishing declared experimental partitions from claims of natural historical divisions. Check that overlapping schemes remain a live possibility and that anti-leakage rules do not silently determine which causal threads deserve attention.

The paragraph supplies the conceptual setup needed for the next one. It could be shorter, but the example is doing explanatory work rather than merely naming “periodization” as a generic concern. | **Medium.** Shortening the historical example could remove the interleaving of economic, political, and cultural causes that makes periodization genuinely ambiguous. Prefer retaining it or trimming only redundant connective language. Check that at least two causal threads still cross the proposed boundary and that the example does more than announce that dates are debatable. |

The contrast is clear. Qualify “any” causal framing if boundaries are mechanically fixed, and recognize that boundaries chosen with known outcomes can leak the evaluator's hindsight into the task selection itself. | **Medium.** Qualifying “any” for mechanical boundaries could mistakenly imply that mechanically chosen dates are framing-neutral; adding hindsight leakage could overshadow the more fundamental target-selection point. Preserve that every partition changes which question is asked, regardless of intent. The paragraph does not specify whether these are separate runs or repeated views within one curriculum. That matters for outcome leakage and fair exposure. Require predefined schemes and state how repeated examples remain comparable. | **Medium.** Choosing separate runs versus overlapping within-curriculum views would settle an experimental design the paragraph leaves open. Preserve comparison among multiple schemes and the intuition that overlapping views counter single-story dependence. Ask the author which implementation is intended; meanwhile list the alternatives and exposure implications without silently selecting one or prohibiting useful retrospective comparisons. |

#### Narrative Lock-In · [L3829](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3829>)

**Goal:** Demonstrate how accumulated historical narratives can become brittle priors and motivate corrective representation.
**Verdict: Partly.** The concrete failure gives this risk more force than a generic caveat, and narrative-status tagging is an actionable idea. However, the account moves from one reported forecast error to a confident causal diagnosis and then generalizes directly to this architecture. Preserve the warning while marking the mechanism as a hypothesis to discriminate.

**Pre-edit risk — Medium.** Causal qualification could make a memorable warning so tentative that its central intuition disappears: a historically coherent Reader may become trapped by yesterday's dominant story. Preserve the concrete forecast, the historian metaphor, and narrative-as-narrative tagging while labeling the mechanism an interpretation to test. Check that the revision distinguishes observed error from inferred cause without reducing the passage to generic forecasting uncertainty or deleting the connection to chronological learning.

Present these as proposed explanations and identify what comparison would separate narrative lock-in from ordinary forecasting error. | **Medium.** Removing “too good a historian” and “could not imagine” would discard the compact intuition that accurate narrative uptake can impair anticipation of discontinuity. Keep the metaphor explicitly as an interpretive warning rather than a diagnosis established by one error. Add a discriminating comparison; check that causal caution does not erase the distinctive narrative-lock-in hypothesis. |

Yet “applies directly,” “will develop,” and “actively sabotages” overstate an untested transfer. It is useful to ask whether the Core induces that distinction. Calling contrarian examples “necessary” is stronger than the reasoning supports; frame them as one candidate control alongside explicit narrative-status tagging and forecast calibration. | **Medium.** Downgrading contrarian events from necessary to optional could lose the educational intuition that reversals challenge narrative certainty. Retain them as a serious candidate and preserve “the pattern so far” as the conceptual target. Distinguish authorial judgment about importance from demonstrated necessity, and check that narrative tagging does not become a mere format proxy for epistemic humility. |

#### The Recency Gradient · [L3837](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3837>)

**Goal:** Identify unequal historical context depth and consider whether reannotation can correct it.
**Verdict: Partly.** The section isolates a distinctive risk of an accumulating graph, then usefully distinguishes explicit graph memory from Teacher parametric knowledge. Its opening treats more context as guaranteed quality, and its proposed remedy does not explain how a hindsight-informed trace can avoid transferring hindsight to the Student. This is an unresolved curriculum specification issue.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Repairing the hindsight claim could force strict temporal purity and remove the intuition that mature understanding improves a second reading of earlier material. Preserve the early/late context asymmetry, Teacher parametric-knowledge distinction, and genuine trade-off between contemporary judgment and retrospective richness. Have the author choose or explicitly compare curriculum contracts. Check that filters or separate traces do not silently prohibit the multipass proposal, and that richer context is presented as a plausible benefit rather than guaranteed quality.

Dense graphs can also introduce irrelevant retrieval or accumulated error, as later limitations acknowledge. State the expected asymmetry in available context, then make annotation quality and bias separate quantities to measure. | **Medium.** Separating context availability from quality could overcorrect into agnosticism and lose the expected benefit of cumulative understanding. Preserve the directional hypothesis that later annotations may be richer because the same Reader has learned more. Qualify certainty and add countervailing graph risks briefly; verify that the paragraph still motivates examining early-era disadvantage rather than merely counting graph nodes. |

The conditional phrasing is appropriate. An era-stratified quality measure would make the consequence easier to test, but the causal concern is already specific enough to guide one. | **Low.** Adding era-stratified metrics could turn a conceptual recency concern into a depth-count proxy. Retain the distinction between parametric knowledge and addressable prior interpretation, and between structurally valid and contextually connected traces. Treat the measure as a diagnostic rather than the definition of historical understanding; check quality judgments remain semantic. |

Specify a horizon filter, separate retrospective and contemporary traces, or explicitly define this as a different curriculum condition. Without that contract the remedy undermines the chronology being evaluated. | **High.** A horizon filter or separate condition changes the proposal's meaning if retrospective enrichment is the intended point. Preserve the intuition that a Reader can understand early texts better after later reading, alongside the risk of transferring hindsight through traces. Ask the author to choose the intended curriculum contract; do not silently impose strict purity. Check that both the benefit and cost survive. |

### Trace Quality, Utility, and Causal Relevance · [L3853](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3853>)

**Goal:** Identify independent points where plausible Teacher traces fail to become useful, safe Student behavior.
**Verdict: Partly.** Depth, usefulness, causal influence, cultural scope, and Teacher dependence form a strong diagnostic decomposition. The graph material interrupts that chain, and some mitigations test a narrower proxy than their named risk. Consolidate overlapping quality caveats and ensure every test link states exactly which failure it can detect and which remains open.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Consolidating quality caveats or moving graph risks could erase different failure layers: expert depth, learning utility, causal governance, cultural assumptions, graph-mediated inertia, and shared Teacher/evaluator blind spots. Preserve each dependency and its possible independent failure. Prefer a dependency map and cross-references before deletion. Check that test-specific limits do not define the entire concern, particularly that semantic quality and formative character remain broader than a rubric score or downstream task improvement.

It also promises assumptions about scaling while the next subsection centers depth, utility, and causal influence. Name those dependencies directly so the reader sees the diagnostic chain before entering it. | **Low.** Replacing the broad transition with the three named dependencies could narrow the promised inquiry to currently operationalized tests. Keep the admission of uncertainty about thought and scaling, then preview depth, utility, and causation as focal dependencies. Check that the sentence introduces a useful organization without claiming those three exhaust the proposal's assumptions. |

#### The Assumption Chain: Depth, Utility, Causation · [L3857](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3857>)

**Goal:** Separate trace depth, training utility, and causal influence so success at one cannot stand in for another.
**Verdict: Met.** This is one of the section's strongest conceptual structures. Each link can fail independently, and the utility paragraph prevents trace visibility from being mistaken for transparency. The final link needs a sharper distinction between causal influence of training data and causal use of emitted traces, especially for model-only deployment.

**Pre-edit risk — High.** Separating training-data effects from emitted-trace faithfulness is necessary but could redefine success so that any behavior improvement counts as the contemplated causal character. Preserve the stronger aspiration that evaluation governs action, the weaker usefulness claim, and the possibility of decorative thought. Add a regime-by-claim distinction, explicitly leaving subjective care unresolved. Verify that model-only internalization is allowed without treating absence of visible reasoning as proof either of faithful deliberation or of its irrelevance.

Chain-of-thought success is only indirect motivation, which the paragraph broadly acknowledges. A reference to the human-baseline gate would help connect this first dependency to the roadmap rather than leaving it as a general uncertainty. | **Low.** Linking to a human-baseline gate could make expert ratings appear to settle what thoughtful evaluation is. Preserve the examples of subtle methodology, unstated assumptions, and cross-field synthesis, plus uncertainty about industrial scale. Add the reference as one diagnostic; check that neither expert resemblance nor chain-of-thought success becomes a sufficient definition of depth. |
| [U493 · L3861](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3861>) · “Second, utility…” | Separate high-quality-looking traces from beneficial Student learning. | Met | This paragraph identifies marginal benefit and negative interference as distinct outcomes, then correctly limits the value of inspectability. It allows modest useful gains without requiring the proposal's strongest aspirations to succeed. The argument would be even sharper if “valuable” were explicitly conditioned on matched capability and the additional annotation/training cost. | **Medium.** Conditioning value on matched capability and cost could subordinate inspectability or safer judgment to a single efficiency threshold. Preserve that modest gains and intermediate artifacts may be independently worthwhile, while beneficial language does not prove disposition. But the paragraph blends training-data causality with inference-time trace faithfulness: a Student can internalize useful policies without its emitted commentary being causal. Separate those claims and their interventions, then specify which is required by each deployment regime and by the stronger character claim. | **High.** Separating training causality from trace causality could weaken the central intuition that thought should steer action into the weaker claim that training data changes behavior. Preserve decorative reasoning as a failure, the stronger governing-disposition aspiration, and the limited analogy to deliberative alignment. Add explicit regime distinctions without replacing the author’s strongest aim; check that subjective authenticity remains unanswered rather than operationally defined away. |

#### Factory Wisdom · [L3871](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3871>)

**Goal:** Identify synthetic ritual as a failure of the intended significance-responsive reading process.
**Verdict: Met.** The subsection names a mechanism, connects two routes to the same symptom, and links an intervention to a significance-sensitive test. It successfully makes a speculative failure falsifiable. Its historical priority sentence is expendable, and the rhythm test should not be allowed to stand in for semantic usefulness or action-level causality.

**Pre-edit risk — Medium.** Trimming historical context or focusing on significance ratings could reduce factory wisdom to poor timing and erase the deeper inversion from discovery to industrialized performance. Preserve performative messiness, inert Core boilerplate, Teacher-to-Student transmission, and the distinction between meaningful hesitation and ritual. If removing the priority sentence, retain its cross-edition provenance elsewhere when relevant. Check that Test 8b is described as probing a symptom, not certifying insight or causal governance.

It distinguishes this from recurrence habituation while linking their shared symptom. Keep the named test links, but avoid letting the vivid “factory wisdom” label imply that messiness itself is the desirable training target. | **Low.** Clarifying that messiness is not itself desirable could flatten the intuition that real discovery has hesitation and revision absent from polished efficiency traces. Preserve that contrast and distinguish responsive messiness from its manufactured imitation. Check that the revision does not implicitly prefer sanitized traces or treat uniform neatness as the safe alternative. |

The opening priority claim does not advance the limitation analysis and can be removed.
